we have |F_R(z_n)-f_n|<=e_R and e_R->0 as R->infinity. If Z=0, every
penalty and error vanishes exactly; D_R=K x {0}. All these formulas also
cover degenerate K and M=0, although a positive terminal value forces
M>0.

For a fully explicit sufficient choice to make e_R<=varepsilon>0, put

    C=M Z sqrt(2S+1)(Pi_n-1), E=(S+1/2)Z^2,
    R>=max(1,2C/varepsilon,sqrt(2E/varepsilon)).

This is an analytic parameter choice, not a computational replay.

## 5. Normalization and equality of worst-case suprema

Let r=||x_0||>0. If f_n>0, choose R so that e_R<f_n. Then F_R(z_n)>0.
The initial z_0 is not a minimizer: otherwise its projection gradient
would be0 and every future iterate would be z_0 with value0. The OWN
closest-minimum radius

    r_R=dist(z_0,D_R^polar)

is therefore positive. A closest minimizer exists because the minimum
set is nonempty, closed and convex in finite dimension. Since z_star is
one of its members,0<r_R<=r. Thus

    2F_R(z_n)/r_R^2 >= 2(f_n-e_R)/r^2.

For any desired eta>0 take e_R<min(f_n,eta*r^2/2). This proves the
normalized approximation claim. If the original terminal value is0,
the desired nonnegative lower comparison is trivial and needs no ratio
with a zero lifted radius.

Let W_n^poly(h) be the same supremum as W_n(h), restricted to functions
F_D(z)=max_(v in D)(<z,v>-||v||^2/2), where D is ANY0-containing compact
finite polytope in ANY finite Euclidean dimension, and the original
minimum/initial-point choices remain available. Every such F_D is in
the original smooth-convex class and has a minimizer, so

    W_n^poly(h)<=W_n(h).

For every admissible original positive-score run and every eta>0,
the preceding lift gives an admissible polytope run with score at least
that original score minus eta. Taking suprema gives the reverse inequality.
The zero-score case does not affect it. Therefore the candidate theorem is

    W_n^poly(h)=W_n(h) for EVERY n and EVERY finite h>=0.

For L>0 apply the construction to(f-f_star)/L after translation, then
multiply F_D by L. The update h_i/L times its gradient is the same
normalized update, and the ratio2(f_n-f_star)/(L r^2) is unchanged.
This includes the n=0 case, all zero steps, and arbitrary finite huge steps.

## 6. Why this is NOT a star-tight or centered-body reduction

Relative to the origin, a projection-gradient function F_D has
F_D(z)=<z,v>-||v||^2/2 at v=P_D(z), hence its outgoing optimum row about
the origin is tight. But choosing that origin as the minimizer here would
charge the irrelevant large coordinate R in the initial radius.

Relative to the actual nearby minimizer z_star=(0,-R), the outgoing
optimum slack for the lifted query is EXACTLY

    <z_i-z_star,grad F_R(z_i)>-F_R(z_i)-||grad F_R(z_i)||^2/2
       =-<z_star,grad F_R(z_i)>=p(g_R(X_i,t_i)).

This is generally positive. Thus the lift preserves the original type
of positive conjugate height; it does NOT remove it for free. The whole
polar minimum cone is load-bearing. Replacing D_R by a body with0 in
its relative interior, forcing the minimum to be0, or fixing the added
coordinate destroys the proved normalization/trajectory argument.

The new contribution, if confirmed, is a free-coordinate, true-radius
finite-polytope realization of every finite GD transcript in supremum.
The previous direct fixed-slice epigraph-distance construction did not
by itself give this ordinary lifted-GD statement. No novelty claim against
the external literature is made; no literature theorem is needed beyond
the explicitly accepted interpolation inequalities.

This result does not choose a hard run from h. Its constants depend on
the given finite trajectory, and Pi_n can be enormous. It provides no
dimension-uniform or R-uniform complexity assertion. The original exact
Zhang--Jiang minimax comparison remains OPEN.

# Fresh sparse/simplex allocation handoff

**JOINT UNVERIFIED candidate: Mechanism 1 passes; Mechanism 2 closes
quantitatively. Original exact-profile target remains OPEN.**

Source: complete frozen `MAIN_CANDIDATE_UNVERIFIED.md`, 6752 bytes,
SHA256 `cd09d624628958d04d5728ca7fd6b0e8fa489ea8c72021d2d3151b8331870963`.

self-contained derivation: `RESULT_UNVERIFIED.md` in this directory.

For a word with `p` strictly positive steps, the strongest resulting
candidate is equality of unrestricted `W_n(h)` with the supremum over
projection-gradient functions of genuine simplices having zero as a
vertex, at most `p+2<=n+2` vertices, and dimension at most `p+1<=n+1`.
Keep the entire polar minimum cone and the function's own closest radius.
For a fixed strict run, use exactly `m+1` vertices in dimension `m`, where
`m` is its number of distinct nonzero sampled gradients including terminal.
No minimality claim for this dimension bound is made.

Load-bearing derivations:

1. The sparse identity is exact:

       <z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>
         =Q_ji-(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R^2.

   Equal gradients force equal heights and zero rows. Strict smoothness
   gives `Q_ji<=-gamma||g_j-g_i||^2` for distinct gradients. With `A` from
   the source and `R^2>=max(1,2A)`, all distinct-label rows are at most
   `-gamma delta^2/2`, where `delta` is the minimum separation of distinct
   gradient labels including zero.

2. Collapse repeated vertices but RETAIN every physical sample. Let
   `w_a=(v_a,epsilon e_a)`, with `e_0=0`, and start the new coordinate block
   at zero. Its ACTUAL position is `-epsilon b_i`, where
   `b_i=sum_{t<i}h_t e_{a(t)}`. The normal correction is exactly

       -epsilon^2 <b_i+e_a,e_b-e_a> <= epsilon^2(S+1).

   Same-label rows stay exactly zero; zero-gradient rows only improve.
   Choosing `epsilon^2<=gamma delta^2/[4(S+1)]` preserves all other guards.

3. The perturbed function values are exactly

       f_i-(eta_i H_i+eta_i^2/2)/R^2-epsilon^2 beta_i,

   with `beta_i=0` for zero gradients and otherwise
   `beta_i=b_{i,a(i)}+1/2<=S+1/2`. The proof gives simultaneous explicit
   choices of `R` and positive `epsilon` for any desired value tolerance.

4. The independent new components make a genuine simplex. Orthogonal
   compression to its vertex span preserves values and GD dynamics, and
   preserves the OWN closest-minimum distance exactly. The projected
   nearby minimizer has initial distance at most the original radius.

5. Scaling an arbitrary original function to `c f`, `c<1`, gives explicit
   finite-run value convergence as `c` tends to one. Choose `c`, then its
   strict-run lift parameters, then compress. This yields equality of
   SUPREMA, without attainment or any uniform-in-word limit claim.

General `L` uses `L F_D`. Zero steps, empty words, repeated gradients at
different times, zero-gradient suffixes, optimum rows, degenerate bodies,
huge finite steps, and positive-radius division are all checked in the
full result. Positive outgoing-optimum slack about the nearby minimizer
is preserved exactly as `eta_i`.

# Sparse projection-body lift from strict smoothness

MAIN-AUTHORED UNVERIFIED candidate extension. Original target OPEN.
This is separate from the frozen epigraph-body draft092bbe1d..., whose
independent audit found no gap at its declared scope but which is not a fact.
No mathematical program or formal call was used for this derivation.

## 1. Exact horizontal lift of one strict-smoothness run

Let f be convex, differentiable and ell-smooth for0<ell<1, with minimum0
attained at0. Fix any finite nonnegative word h of length n and its actual
jets x_i,g_i,f_i, i=0,...,n. Define

    eta_i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    H_i=sum_(t<i)h_t eta_t,
    Q_ji=f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_j-g_i||^2/2.

Include a reference label star with g_star=eta_star=f_star=x_star=0.
The standard ell-smooth interpolation inequality gives

    Q_ji <= -gamma||g_j-g_i||^2,
    gamma=(1-ell)/(2ell)>0.

The optimum rows imply eta_i>=gamma||g_i||^2>=0, and
f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=gamma||g_i||^2. If g_i=g_j, both convexity inequalities
give f_i-f_j=<g_i,x_i-x_j>, hence eta_i=eta_j. Thus an equal-gradient pair
has no eta difference even if its original positions differ. If g_i=0,
then f_i=eta_i=0.

For a finite R>0 let

    v_i=(g_i,eta_i/R),
    D_R=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n},
    z_i=(x_i,-R-H_i/R),
    z_star=(0,-R).

This body has AT MOST n+2 vertices and lies in one extra coordinate.
Every eta_i>=0, so z_star belongs to the whole minimum cone D_R^polar of
F_R(z)=max_(v in D_R)(<z,v>-||v||^2/2). Its selected initial distance is
exactly||x_0||.

The exact normal-condition identity for any sampled i and any j including
star is

    <z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>
      =Q_ji-(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R^2.             (1)

For the star label, interpret v_star=0; no z_star term enters this normal
identity. If g_i=g_j, both Q_ji and the eta difference vanish, so the
normal condition is exactly0. Otherwise -Q_ji is strictly positive.

Define the finite nonnegative number

    A=max over sampled i and j in{star,0,...,n} with g_i!=g_j of
      (H_i+eta_i)(eta_i-eta_j)_+/(-Q_ji),

using max empty=0. Then any R>0 with R^2>=A makes EVERY expression in(1)
nonpositive. Since it is affine in v_j, it is nonpositive on all of D_R.
Therefore P_(D_R)(z_i)=v_i at every original physical index, including n.
No normal condition is replaced by an expected or local row.

The literal dynamics are exactly

    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i*v_i,

because the original horizontal update is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i and the
vertical sum increases by h_i eta_i. Thus the SAME ordinary GD word on
one static F_R reproduces the ORIGINAL horizontal trajectory exactly.

At each original sample the actual value is

    F_R(z_i)=f_i-[eta_i H_i+eta_i^2/2]/R^2.          (2)

This follows by expanding <z_i,v_i>-||v_i||^2/2. The value correction is
nonnegative and tends to0. Choosing R even larger retains a positive
terminal value whenever f_n>0. The actual closest radius is then positive
and at most||x_0||, exactly as in the epigraph-body argument. Therefore
the own-closest normalized terminal score is at least

    2[f_n-(eta_n H_n+eta_n^2/2)/R^2]/||x_0||^2.

The body may be degenerate, vertices may repeat, and it is NOT yet claimed
to be a simplex. For every fixed finite run, the maximum defining A is
finite. This is not a uniform in-word conditioning estimate.

## 2. From general1-smooth functions to strict smoothness

Given an arbitrary original1-smooth f with minimum0 at0, choose0<c<1 and
run GD with the SAME physical word h on f_c=c f. Its smoothness constant is
c<1, and its minimum set is unchanged. Its actual trajectory converges
to the original one as c increases to1, by finite induction and continuity
of the gradient. The horizontal trajectories are not asserted identical
at this regularization stage; exactness in Section1 refers to the f_c run.

An explicit comparison is available. With original
M=max_(0<=i<=n)||g_i||, Pi_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), the position error obeys

    ||x_i^c-x_i|| <= (1-c)M(Pi_i-1).

Indeed subtract the two updates and bound
||c grad f(x_i^c)-grad f(x_i)|| by c||x_i^c-x_i||+(1-c)||g_i||.
Smoothness and convexity give
|f(x_n^c)-f(x_n)|<=||g_n|| ||x_n^c-x_n||+||x_n^c-x_n||^2/2.
Thus f_c(x_n^c)->f_n. No infimum/limit interchange is required: choose c
for a particular original run and tolerance, then a finite R for its
strict-smoothness run.

This would show that the unrestricted W_n(h) equals the supremum over
projection-body functions generated by at most n+2 points including0,
with their actual complete minimum sets, for every fixed finite h>=0.
The conclusion is again equality of suprema, not finite exact attainment
for arbitrary1-smooth f, and not a schedule-only hard-instance selector.

General L uses L F_R after normalization. Zero steps, repeated samples,
zero gradients and terminal zero values must be handled explicitly in a
complete independent proof. The no-positive-score case is trivial and
must not introduce a closest-radius0 division.

## 3. Optional second mechanism: make the nonzero vertices independent

This paragraph is a PROPOSAL, not an established part of the above proof.
Choose R^2>A strictly. Distinct-gradient pairs then have strict normal
inequalities. Collapse repeated vectors and perturb the distinct nonzero
vertices by tiny separate orthogonal components; retain0 as a vertex.
Use identical perturbations for every occurrence of one repeated vector,
start the added coordinates at0, and recompute ALL actual lifted positions
by the same physical word. The nearby minimum keeps those added coordinates0.
Its dot product with a perturbed vertex is still -eta_i<0 whenever g_i!=0.

The hoped-for argument is that finitely many strict normal inequalities
survive a sufficiently small perturbation; duplicate-vertex inequalities
are exactly0 throughout. The perturbed body would then be a genuine simplex
with0 as a vertex. Objective values, initial distance and full chronological
trajectory would need a quantitative continuity proof. Projecting to the
span of these vertices can only lower the selected initial radius and
preserves their inner products, but must be accounted for explicitly.

Do not call this proposal proved just because the normal matrix is finite.
Check zero-gradient samples, endpoint value positivity, equal-gradient
occurrences at different times, complete minimum sets, dimension count and
whether any supposedly strict constraint is actually active. If it closes,
state the strongest correct finite-vertex/simplex universality theorem.
Otherwise retain Sections1--2 separately and exhibit the exact failed step.

No version of this representation selects a hard original run from h or
compares its score to the exact all-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark. The
original minimax Goal remains OPEN.

# Finite inverse selection with unequal vertex norms

MAIN-AUTHORED UNVERIFIED analytical advisory, not a fact or a new allocation.
The native sparse/simplex result is separately reported and remains unverified.
The following elementary identity is useful for subsequent geometric search.

Let D=conv{v_0,...,v_k} be any fixed finite convex hull and h>1. Given a
future position y, choose a vertex index s maximizing the finite scores

    score_s=<y,v_s>+(h-1)||v_s||^2/2.

Put x=y+h v_s. For every vertex v_u,

    <x-v_s,v_u-v_s>
      =score_u-score_s-(h-1)||v_u-v_s||^2/2 <=0.

Therefore P_D(x)=v_s and x-h P_D(x)=y. This is an exact finite vertex
preimage selector without the equal-norm hypothesis. It applies to the
actual projection gradient of one static F_D and may be iterated backward
in the original word when every h_i>1. The zero vertex, if present, is
one ordinary candidate in the score maximum. Ties cause no legality issue.

This is only a legality construction. It gives NO useful initial-distance
bound, NO terminal gain and NO original benchmark comparison. Minimization
of the initial radius among all valid preimages is not implied by this
max-score rule. A chosen terminal point and all vertices must still come
from an explicit schedule-selected law before the forward run, and the
full polar minimum cone must be used. In particular, do not read the
simplex universality theorem candidate as already providing a hard selector.

The main fully read native27346-byte RESULT_UNVERIFIED.md, SHA256
29095a40ec7ffc4a27a2a5ada51719542f8b9359ca978b768970dd37eb855f72.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000553 -->
# Joint candidate: sparse exact lifts and simplex universality for finite GD words

**JOINT UNVERIFIED THEOREM CANDIDATE.** Analytical result of the ONE fresh
bounded allocation in this directory. The sparse main-authored mechanism
survives independent checking, and the proposed simplex extension admits the
explicit proof below. Neither result is a verifier-accepted project fact.
The original exact Zhang--Jiang minimax target remains OPEN.

The complete frozen source was `MAIN_CANDIDATE_UNVERIFIED.md`, 6752 bytes,
actual SHA256
`cd09d624628958d04d5728ca7fd6b0e8fa489ea8c72021d2d3151b8331870963`.
It was read completely and was not edited. The earlier epigraph audit is not
used as mathematical authority; the needed elementary facts are derived here.

## 1. Strongest conclusion established by this analytical argument

Fix an integer `n>=0` and a finite word `h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1})>=0`. Let

    S = sum_{t<n} h_t,
    p = #{t<n : h_t>0}.

Normalize smoothness to one. Let `W_n(h)` be the original unrestricted
worst-case normalized terminal objective. Define `W_n^simplex(h)` by
restricting its functions to

    F_D(q) = max_{v in D} { <q,v>-||v||^2/2 },

where, in some Euclidean dimension `m` with `1<=m<=p+1`,

    D = conv{0,w_1,...,w_m},

and `w_1,...,w_m` are linearly independent. Thus `D` is a genuine
full-dimensional `m`-simplex and zero is one of its vertices. Keep arbitrary
initial points and the complete actual minimum set, with the original choice
of minimizer in the denominator. The candidate conclusion is

    W_n^simplex(h) = W_n(h).                        (1.1)

Equivalently, every positive-score original finite run can be approximated
from below in normalized terminal score by such a simplex function under
the SAME unmodified GD word. The new run can be normalized by its OWN closest
minimizer. There are at most `p+2<=n+2` vertices, in dimension at most
`p+1<=n+1`, independently of the original ambient dimension.

More precisely, for a given strict-smoothness run, `m` can be taken to be its
number of distinct nonzero sampled gradients, including the terminal sample.
The construction gives exactly `m+1` simplex vertices and dimension `m`.
No claim that this dimension bound is universally minimal is made.

For an arbitrary original 1-smooth run, first use `c f` with `c<1` close to
one. For that strict-smoothness run, the pre-compression lift reproduces all
ORIGINAL HORIZONTAL ITERATES exactly and has an explicit value correction.
Compression preserves the lifted values and GD dynamics; it need not retain
the original horizontal coordinates as a distinguished coordinate block.

Equation (1.1) is equality of SUPREMA. It is not finite-parameter exact
representation of an arbitrary 1-smooth run or attainment within the simplex
class. It neither chooses an adverse original run from `h` nor compares the
score with `U_n^ZJ`. At general smoothness `L`, the restricted class is
`L F_D`, with step `h_i/L`.

## 2. Elementary projection facts and the complete minimum set

For a nonempty compact convex set `D`, the maximizer defining `F_D` is unique:
completing the square identifies it with the unique nearest point `P_D(q)`.
For `v in D`, the exact characterization is

    P_D(q)=v  iff  <q-v,w-v><=0 for every w in D.     (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating the squared distance on the segment
from `v` to `w` at zero. For sufficiency, expand

    ||q-w||^2 = ||q-v||^2 + ||w-v||^2
                -2<q-v,w-v> >= ||q-v||^2.

For a finite convex hull it is enough to check every generating point,
because the left side of (2.1) is affine in `w`.

The two normal inequalities for `v=P_D(q)` and `v'=P_D(q')` imply

    <q-q',v-v'> >= ||v-v'||^2.

Thus the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the affine maxima at each
other's optimizers gives

    0 <= F_D(q')-F_D(q)-<P_D(q),q'-q>
      <= <P_D(q')-P_D(q),q'-q> <= ||q'-q||^2.

Consequently `F_D` is differentiable with gradient `P_D` and is globally
1-smooth. It is convex as a maximum of affine functions of `q` and finite
because `D` is compact.

When `0 in D`, the minimum is zero and its COMPLETE minimum set is the cone

    C_D = { a : <a,v><=0 for every v in D }.          (2.2)

Membership makes every defining expression nonpositive, while `v=0` gives
zero. Conversely, if `<a,v>>0` for some `v in D`, then `theta v in D` for
`0<=theta<=1`, and

    <a,theta v>-||theta v||^2/2 > 0

for sufficiently small positive `theta`. Thus `a` is not a minimizer.
The polar here always means the cone with threshold ZERO, not a body polar
with threshold one. It is nonempty, closed, and convex, so every point has
a closest point in it. Existence follows, for example, by restricting a
distance-minimizing sequence to a bounded ball and taking a convergent
subsequence.

## 3. Strict smoothness supplies the necessary strict rows

Let `0<ell<1`, let `f` be convex differentiable and ell-smooth, and translate
and subtract its chosen optimum so that `f(0)=min f=0`. Use its ACTUAL GD
samples `i=0,...,n`, including the terminal sample, with

    x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i g_i,
    g_i=grad f(x_i),  f_i=f(x_i).

Add a reference label `*` with `x_*=g_*=f_*=0`. Define

    Q_ji = f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_j-g_i||^2/2,
    gamma = (1-ell)/(2ell)>0.

The ell-smooth interpolation necessity is

    Q_ji <= -gamma ||g_j-g_i||^2.                   (3.1)

This is the smoothness-scaled necessity part of accepted local fact
`1252b8823bb5a290`; none of its duality or attainment conclusions is used.
One can also derive the precise orientation directly: the convex function
`k(x)=f(x)-<g_j,x>` has a minimum at `x_j`, and an ell-smooth descent step
from `x_i` of length `1/ell` gives

    k(x_j) <= k(x_i-(g_i-g_j)/ell)
             <= k(x_i)-||g_i-g_j||^2/(2ell).

Rearrange and add the `||g_i-g_j||^2/2` term to obtain (3.1).

Put

    eta_i = <x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    H_i = sum_{t<i} h_t eta_t,
    eta_* = 0.

The optimum row `Q_{i,*}=-eta_i` gives

    eta_i >= gamma ||g_i||^2 >= 0,                 (3.2)

and `Q_{*,i}=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2` gives

    f_i-||g_i||^2/2 >= gamma ||g_i||^2.             (3.3)

In particular every nonzero gradient has STRICTLY positive `eta_i`.
If `g_i=0`, convexity makes `x_i` a minimizer, so `f_i=eta_i=0`.
All later gradients and positions then remain zero-gradient/stationary.

If `g_i=g_j=g`, the two elementary convexity inequalities force

    f_i-f_j=<g,x_i-x_j>.

Therefore `eta_i=eta_j`, and direct substitution gives `Q_ji=0`.
The same conclusion applies to equal zero gradients and to the reference
label. Distinct positions with the same gradient need not be identified as
positions; only their gradient/height vertex labels will be identified.

## 4. Mechanism 1: exact sparse normal-guard construction

For `R>0`, define

    v_i=(g_i,eta_i/R),  v_*=0,
    D_R=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n},
    z_i=(x_i,-R-H_i/R),
    a_R=(0,-R).

This is a compact finite polytope in one extra coordinate, with at most
`n+2` generating points. The point `a_R` belongs to its full minimum cone
because `<a_R,v_i>=-eta_i<=0`. For `z_0=(x_0,-R)`,

    ||z_0-a_R||=||x_0||.                           (4.1)

The claimed identity is exact. For every physical sample `i` and every
`j in {*,0,...,n}`,

    <z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>
      = <x_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>-(eta_j-eta_i)
        -(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R^2
      = Q_ji-(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R^2.          (4.2)

For clarity, the middle terms expand to

    <x_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>-(eta_j-eta_i)
      = f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_j-g_i||^2/2.

This checks both the orientation `Q_ji` and the sign of the vertical
correction. No position associated with the reference `*` appears in the
normal test other than through the zero jet in `Q_*i`.

If `g_i=g_j`, both terms in (4.2) vanish. Otherwise `-Q_ji>0` by (3.1).
Define the data-dependent finite number

    A = max_{i=0,...,n; j in {*,0,...,n}; g_i!=g_j}
          (H_i+eta_i)(eta_i-eta_j)_+ / (-Q_ji),      (4.3)

with maximum over an empty set equal to zero. Finiteness follows from
finitely many finite data and strictly positive denominators. Here
`H_i+eta_i>=0` uses the nonnegative steps.

For `R^2>=A`, all expressions in (4.2) are nonpositive. By (2.1),

    P_{D_R}(z_i)=v_i,  for EVERY physical i=0,...,n. (4.4)

The prospective positions satisfy

    z_{i+1}=z_i-h_i v_i,                            (4.5)

because `H_{i+1}=H_i+h_i eta_i`. Equations (4.4)--(4.5), by induction from
the stated initial point, prove that these are the ACTUAL ordinary GD
iterates on the single fixed function `F_{D_R}`. All original horizontal
positions are reproduced exactly. The added coordinate moves by
`-h_i eta_i/R`; it is not held fixed.

The value at every actual sample is also exact:

    F_{D_R}(z_i)
      = <z_i,v_i>-||v_i||^2/2
      = f_i-C_i/R^2,
    C_i = eta_i H_i+eta_i^2/2 >= 0.                (4.6)

The optimum/reference row is included in the guard; it is not left to a
heuristic value estimate. If `g_i!=0`, (4.4) already implies positive value
via `F_{D_R}(z_i)>=||v_i||^2/2>0`. Taking `R` larger also makes the explicit
correction in (4.6) as small as desired.

This completes Mechanism 1 with no gap found. If only `R^2=A` is used, some
distinct-vertex rows may be equalities; that boundary is insufficient for
the generic perturbation proposal. The next section deliberately chooses
strict quantitative slack instead.

## 5. Quantitative strict margins, including zero-gradient samples

Suppose at least one sampled gradient is nonzero. List the distinct nonzero
gradient values as `g^1,...,g^m`, with their well-defined heights
`eta^1,...,eta^m`. Write

    g^0=0,  eta^0=0,
    a(i) in {0,...,m},
    g_i=g^{a(i)},  eta_i=eta^{a(i)}.

The equality of heights proved above makes this label map unambiguous,
including repeated gradients at different physical times. Let

    delta = min_{0<=a<b<=m} ||g^a-g^b|| > 0.        (5.1)

Choose

    R^2 >= max(1,2A).                              (5.2)

For a physical `i` and a target label `b`, define

    N_{ib}=<z_i-v^{a(i)},v^b-v^{a(i)}>,
    v^b=(g^b,eta^b/R).

When `a(i)!=b`, choose any original representative of `b` (or the reference
label for zero) in (4.2). The possible positive correction is at most
`A(-Q_ji)/R^2<=(-Q_ji)/2`. Thus

    N_{ib} <= Q_ji/2
            <= -(gamma/2)||g_i-g^b||^2
            <= -gamma delta^2/2.                  (5.3)

For equal labels it is identically zero. This retains every PHYSICAL
sample row, even when one vertex label occurs repeatedly; those samples
have different `H_i` and must not be silently collapsed as time indices.

If `g_i=0` and `b!=0`, the exact formula is even more transparent:

    N_{ib}=Q_{ji}-H_i eta^b/R^2<0.

So zero-gradient samples supply strict inequalities against every nonzero
vertex. Their equality against zero itself is a harmless identical-vertex
row, not a missing strictness condition.

The strict margins also show that each distinct nonzero `v^b` is an exposed
vertex of `D_R`: at an occurrence of label `b`, the vector `z_i-v^b`
strictly exposes it among all other generating points. Zero is exposed by
`a_R`, since all `eta^b>0`. Thus, under (5.2), the sparse body already has
exactly `m+1` vertices, although they need not be affinely independent.

## 6. Mechanism 2: an explicit simplex perturbation with exact dynamics

Append `m` new mutually orthogonal coordinates. Let `e_1,...,e_m` be their
standard basis and set `e_0=0`. For a number `epsilon>0`, define

    w^b=(v^b,epsilon e_b),  0<=b<=m,
    D_{R,epsilon}=conv{0,w^1,...,w^m}.

In particular `w^0=0`; a zero-gradient vertex is NOT given a new nonzero
component. Every occurrence of the same nonzero gradient receives exactly
the same component. Define cumulative chronological vectors

    b_i=sum_{t<i} h_t e_{a(t)},
    Z_i=(z_i,-epsilon b_i),
    A_R=(a_R,0).

The INITIAL appended coordinates are zero. Their subsequent values above
are recomputed from all the actual preceding steps, rather than assumed to
stay zero. By construction,

    Z_0=(x_0,-R,0),
    Z_{i+1}=Z_i-h_i w^{a(i)}.                       (6.1)

The normal condition has an explicit perturbation identity. With
`a=a(i)`, for every target label `b`,

    <Z_i-w^a,w^b-w^a>
       = N_{ib}
         -epsilon^2 <b_i+e_a,e_b-e_a>.             (6.2)

There is no term linear in `epsilon`, because old and new coordinate blocks
are orthogonal and the new initial block is zero.

Let `b_{i,a}=<b_i,e_a>` for `a>0`, and take `b_{i,0}=0`. All these numbers
are nonnegative and at most `S`. The coefficient of `epsilon^2` in (6.2)
is exactly

    0                         if a=b;
    b_{i,a}-b_{i,b}+1          if a,b>0 and a!=b;
    b_{i,a}+1                  if a>0 and b=0;
    -b_{i,b}                   if a=0 and b>0.

Hence it is at most `S+1`. Equal-label rows remain exactly zero for every
physical occurrence. At zero-gradient samples the perturbation only makes
the distinct-vertex normal inequality more negative.

Choose, in addition to (5.2),

    0<epsilon^2 <= gamma delta^2/[4(S+1)].          (6.3)

Combining (5.3) and (6.2) gives, for all different labels,

    <Z_i-w^{a(i)},w^b-w^{a(i)}>
         <= -gamma delta^2/4 < 0.                 (6.4)

The identical-label rows are zero. Thus every generator satisfies the normal
condition, and (2.1) proves

    P_{D_{R,epsilon}}(Z_i)=w^{a(i)}

at every physical index, including the terminal. Along with (6.1), this
proves that `Z_i` is the ACTUAL GD run of `F_{D_{R,epsilon}}` under the same
word. This is a quantitative proof of the proposal, not an appeal to
genericity or continuity at a supposedly strict equality.

The vectors `w^1,...,w^m` are linearly independent: projection of a linear
relation to the new coordinate block gives `epsilon` times every coefficient
equal to zero. Therefore this body is a genuine `m`-simplex in its span,
with exactly `m+1` vertices including zero.

The nearby minimum remains exact:

    <A_R,w^b>=-eta^b<0 for b>0,
    ||Z_0-A_R||=||x_0||.                           (6.5)

Thus the whole minimum cone has not been replaced by the origin, and no
large artificial coordinate is paid for in the initial distance.

Finally, every perturbed value has the explicit formula

    F_{D_{R,epsilon}}(Z_i)
      = f_i-C_i/R^2-epsilon^2 beta_i,
    beta_i=<b_i,e_{a(i)}>+||e_{a(i)}||^2/2.         (6.6)

If `a(i)=0`, both `C_i` and `beta_i` are zero and the value is exactly zero.
Otherwise

    beta_i=b_{i,a(i)}+1/2,
    0<beta_i<=S+1/2.

This proves value continuity quantitatively while preserving the original
strict-run horizontal trajectory exactly before compression.

For any desired strict-run terminal value loss `tau>0`, the simultaneous
explicit choices

    R^2 >= max(1,2A,2C_n/tau),
    0<epsilon^2 <= min{
        gamma delta^2/[4(S+1)],
        tau/[2(S+1/2)]
    }                                              (6.7)

make the total loss in (6.6) at most `tau`, while all different-label normal
guards remain strictly negative. Every bound is finite and strictly
positive where a positive perturbation is needed. No uniform lower bound
on `delta`, or uniform upper bound on `A`, is assumed.

## 7. Compression to exactly m dimensions and the own closest radius

The construction so far lives in the original space plus `1+m` coordinates.
Let

    E=span{w^1,...,w^m},  dim E=m,

and let `P_E` be orthogonal projection onto `E`. Since the entire body lies
in `E`, for every `Z` and every `w in D_{R,epsilon}`,

    <Z,w>=<P_E Z,w>,
    ||Z-w||^2=||P_E Z-w||^2+||Z-P_E Z||^2.

Consequently both the value and the selected projection gradient are
preserved:

    F_D(Z)=F_D(P_E Z),
    P_D(Z)=P_D(P_E Z).

Put `q_i=P_E Z_i` and `a=P_E A_R`. Then

    q_{i+1}=q_i-h_i w^{a(i)},
    P_D(q_i)=w^{a(i)},
    F_D(q_i)=F_D(Z_i),
    <a,w^b>=-eta^b<=0.

Thus the compressed points are an actual GD run of the same simplex
function regarded as a function on the `m`-dimensional Euclidean space
`E`; choosing an orthonormal basis identifies it with `R^m`. The point `a`
is a true minimizer, and

    ||q_0-a||=||P_E(Z_0-A_R)||<=||x_0||.            (7.1)

This handles the selected radius. The OWN closest radius is preserved
exactly under the compression as well. Writing `C_E` for the polar cone
inside `E`, the ambient minimum set is

    C_D = C_E + E^perp.

Orthogonal decomposition therefore gives

    dist(Z_0,C_D)=dist(P_E Z_0,C_E).                 (7.2)

If the terminal value is positive, neither starting point is a minimizer:
a zero gradient would leave every following iterate fixed with zero value.
The shared radius in (7.2), denoted `rho`, is then positive, and (7.1) gives

    0<rho<=||x_0||.

For `r=||x_0||>0`, if `tau<f_n`, (6.7) consequently yields

    2 F_D(q_n)/rho^2 >= 2(f_n-tau)/r^2.             (7.3)

The positivity of the lower numerator is explicit; no inequality with a
negative numerator is reversed by changing the denominator. Neither the
selected compressed radius nor the closest radius is asserted to equal the
original `r`. The inequality is sufficient.

The number of distinct sampled positions is at most `p+1`, because a zero
step leaves the position unchanged and each positive step can add at most
one position. Therefore

    m <= p+1,
    number of simplex vertices = m+1 <= p+2.

This count includes the terminal gradient. Repeated gradients can reduce
`m` further, even when their positions differ. It is not legitimate to
remove a distinct terminal gradient merely because it is unqueried by the
last update; it remains part of this value/normal construction.

## 8. Regularization of an arbitrary 1-smooth original run

Return now to an arbitrary convex differentiable 1-smooth `f`, with chosen
minimum zero at zero and initial radius `r>0`. Its original samples are
`x_i,g_i,f_i`. For `0<c<1`, define `f_c=c f` and run the SAME word on it,
starting from the same `x_0`. Denote the new actual positions by `x_i^c`.
Its smoothness bound is `ell=c<1`, and its minimum set is unchanged.

Set

    M=max_{0<=i<=n}||g_i||,
    Pi_i=product_{t<i}(1+h_t),
    B_i=(1-c)M(Pi_i-1).

Subtract the two actual updates and use 1-Lipschitz continuity of `grad f`:

    ||x_{i+1}^c-x_{i+1}||
      <= (1+c h_i)||x_i^c-x_i||+(1-c)h_i||g_i||
      <= (1+h_i)||x_i^c-x_i||+(1-c)h_i M.

Induction gives

    ||x_i^c-x_i||<=B_i.                             (8.1)

Convexity and smoothness at the ORIGINAL terminal point imply

    |f(x_n^c)-f_n|<=||g_n||B_n+B_n^2/2.

Therefore the terminal error after scaling the objective is explicitly

    |f_c(x_n^c)-f_n| <= e_c,
    e_c=(1-c)f_n+c||g_n||B_n+c B_n^2/2 -> 0.        (8.2)

For example, with `K=f_n+M^2(Pi_n-1)+M^2(Pi_n-1)^2/2`, one has
`e_c<=(1-c)K`. For a positive original terminal value, `K>0`, so a finite
choice of `c<1` sufficiently close to one is immediate from this bound.

The exact horizontal reproduction in Sections 4--6 is of the ACTUAL
`f_c` run. There is no assertion that this regularized run is horizontally
identical to the original `f` run.

Given any positive original score and `eta>0`, choose

    0<d<min(f_n,eta r^2/2).

First choose `c<1` so that `e_c<d/2`; the new strict-run terminal value is
positive. Then choose a strict-run loss `0<tau<d/2`, and choose `R,epsilon`
by (6.7) for THAT strict run. Its total value loss relative to the original
is less than `d`, so the resulting simplex run has positive terminal value
and own-closest score

    2 F_D(q_n)/rho^2
      >= 2(f_n-d)/r^2
      > 2f_n/r^2-eta.                              (8.3)

This sequence of choices is important: `c` first, then its finite actual
transcript, then `R`, then a positive `epsilon`, then span compression.
The parameters may depend on the instance, the entire fixed word, and the
tolerance. The number of distinct gradient labels may also depend on `c`,
but it always satisfies the same bound `m<=p+1`.

## 9. Equality of suprema, scale L, and boundary audit

Every simplex function in (1.1) is a legal convex differentiable 1-smooth
function with a minimizer, by Section 2. Thus

    W_n^simplex(h)<=W_n(h).

For every original positive-score instance and every tolerance, (8.3)
produces an admissible simplex instance with no more than `p+2` vertices
and dimension no more than `p+1`. Taking suprema gives the reverse
inequality and proves the candidate identity (1.1).

Original zero-score runs do not require a ratio with zero closest radius.
The restricted class is nonempty and has nonnegative scores. It even has
a positive score for every fixed word: in dimension one take `D=[0,1]`
and start at `q_0=1+S`. Every iterate stays at least one, all projection
gradients equal one, and the terminal value is `1/2`, with closest radius
`1+S`. Its score is `1/(1+S)^2>0`.

For arbitrary `L>0`, replace the original function by

    tilde f(x)=[f(x_*+x)-f(x_*)]/L.

The normalized step is `h_i`, exactly corresponding to the original
`h_i/L` step. Multiply the final simplex function by `L`. Its gradient
is `L P_D`, its algorithm again uses the same word, and its normalized
score is still `2F_D(q_n)/rho^2`.

The complete boundary audit is as follows.

1. **Empty word and all-zero words.** Here `p=0`, positions do not move,
   there is at most one nonzero gradient label, and `H_i=b_i=0`. A positive
   score uses a one-dimensional segment simplex. Equations (4.6) and (6.6)
   reduce respectively to losses `eta_i^2/(2R^2)` and `epsilon^2/2`.
   The regularization error (8.2) still applies with `Pi_n=1`.
2. **Zero steps among positive steps.** Duplicate positions and gradients
   use the same label. Both cumulative vectors simply omit their zero
   contributions, and no strictness between identical vertices is required.
3. **Repeated nonzero gradients at different times.** Convexity forces
   equal heights. Use one vertex and one appended coordinate for the whole
   label, but retain every physical normal row and its actual `H_i,b_i`.
   Equation (6.2) is identically zero for a same-label comparison regardless
   of accumulated time. Different-label comparisons have the explicit bound.
4. **Zero-gradient samples.** Their function values and heights are zero,
   and their vertex remains exactly zero. Against nonzero vertices their
   old rows are strict and their new correction is nonpositive. From such
   a sample onward the original and lifted runs are stationary. If the
   terminal score is positive, no zero-gradient sample can have occurred.
5. **All gradients zero.** The run has score zero. The exact sparse lift
   is the point body `{0}`. A zero-dimensional simplex convention can
   describe it, but no own-closest quotient is formed and this case is
   unnecessary for the positive-score supremum argument.
6. **Origin/optimum rows.** The reference zero vertex is included in `A`
   and in every perturbed normal test. Its perturbation coefficient at a
   nonzero sample is `b_{i,a}+1`, the potentially positive term controlled
   by `S+1`. It is not dropped. The chosen nearby minimizer is checked
   separately by its negative inner products with all nonzero vertices.
7. **Degenerate sparse bodies.** Degeneracy before the orthogonal
   perturbation causes no issue. Positive `epsilon` makes all nonzero
   vertices independent. Compression then gives precisely their span,
   without a full-dimensionality assumption on the original gradient data.
8. **Huge finite steps and small strict margins.** `A`, `S`, and `Pi_n` may
   be huge, while `gamma` and `delta` may be tiny. For a fixed finite run
   they remain finite, with `gamma,delta>0`, so (6.7) permits a finite `R`
   and a strictly positive `epsilon`. No word-uniform conditioning estimate
   is asserted, and no limit is interchanged with a supremum or infimum.
9. **Closest-minimum distance.** Positivity is obtained before dividing;
   compression preserves the own closest radius exactly by (7.2), while
   the explicitly chosen minimum gives its upper bound by the original
   radius. No claim of convergence of these radii is needed.

The scope remains nonnegative finite steps and finite horizons. Negative
steps are not covered by the signs used in `H_i`, `b_i`, and the estimates.

## 10. What this does and does not change

The first mechanism improves the earlier general-polytope representation
in a different way: strict smoothness allows finitely many lifted gradient
POINTS to satisfy exact projection guards. Its horizontal strict-run
trajectory is exact, and its value loss is order `R^{-2}` with explicit
data-dependent constants.

The second mechanism is now supported by a complete quantitative analytical
argument. It does not assume an equality becomes strict by genericity. All
genuine equalities are same-vertex rows, kept exactly zero; every other row
has the bound (5.3), and its computed perturbation is controlled by (6.3).
The outcome is a simplex with zero as a vertex in dimension at most
`p+1`, with the complete polar minimum cone still essential.

The representation retains positive outgoing-optimum slack. In the
pre-compression construction, for `w_i=w^{a(i)}`,

    <Z_i-A_R,w_i>-F_D(Z_i)-||w_i||^2/2
       =-<A_R,w_i>=eta_i.

After compression the same identity holds with `q_i,a`, because their inner
products with every vertex are preserved. It is strictly positive for
every nonzero strict-run gradient. Thus this is not an origin-interior or
an optimum-tight reduction about the nearby minimizer. The simplex has
zero as a VERTEX and a substantial polar minimum cone.

Pointwise equality of the two worst-case formulations allows their infima
over `h` to be equated after the proof. It does not give an all-word lower
bound, an improving all-function upper bound, an explicit schedule-only
adversary, or the required comparison with the exact Zhang--Jiang sequence.
That target-level gap is unchanged.

## 11. Provenance, resource accounting, and terminal status

- This is one DISTINCT fresh analytical allocation, following completion
  of the previous audit; it is not an automatic renewal of that allocation.
- At most the TWO assigned mechanisms were pursued: sparse normal-guard
  lifting and quantitative independent-coordinate simplex perturbation.
- The requested model/effort is `gpt-6-astra/ultra` under the fresh
  `ALLOCATION.json`; serving-backend metadata is held by the parent dispatch
  and is not independently exposed within this subagent.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000554 -->
# R12 primal handoff — fixed A/B profiles rejected; C unresolved

**UNVERIFIED direct mathematical draft.** Grant 8298ccaea9198109. Original minimax target OPEN. This is not a verifier-accepted fact or a full-class objective upper bound.

The completed proof is PROOF_ATTEMPT.md in this packet. Its Sections 1--8 give an actual static-function counterexample to both fixed dominance profiles A and B for **every sufficiently large odd literal enclosing depth \(m\)**. Section 10 records the distinct second approach on Profile C and the precise point where its proposed cyclic triangular trajectory fails feasibility.

## Result that the parent can use after checking

Keep exactly the enclosing-\(m\) parameters
\[
\epsilon=1/(4m),\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\]
and the complete physical word \(H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j)\). Let \(S_j=\sum H_j\), \(S=S_m\), \(n=2^m-1\), \(k_j=ac^j\).

Use the one-dimensional static Huber function
\[
F(x)=x^2/2\ (|x|\le1),\qquad F(x)=|x|-1/2\ (|x|\ge1),
\quad g(x)=\operatorname{clip}(x,-1,1).
\]
It is convex and 1-smooth, has unique minimum \(x_\star=0\), and has \(Q_{\star,i}=0\) at every actual sample.

Choose
\[
X_1=\beta,\qquad
X_j=S_{j-1}+1+k_{j-1}-X_{j-1},\quad x_0=X_m.
\]
The proof establishes \(X_j\le k_j\). For each \(j\ge2\), the first child and bridge starting at \(X_j\) have gradient \(+1\) and end at \(-X_{j-1}\); odd symmetry then continues the actual right suffix. The last physical query is \((-1)^{m-1}\beta\), and the real endpoint is
\[
x_n=g_n=(-1)^m q,\qquad q=a\beta.
\]
All \(2^m-1\) physical steps are retained. The proof gives the explicit sample \(x_i\), gradient \(g_i\), and value \(F_i\) at every index. The original radius is \(X_m^2\), and the actual normalized objective value is exactly \(q^2/X_m^2\).

For profile \(P\), write
\[
U_P=\sum_{i<n}u_i^P,\quad G_P=\sum_{i<n}u_i^Pg_i,\quad
u_n^P=B_{\rm cert}-U_P,\quad B_{\rm cert}=1+(32/17)S.
\]
On odd \(m\), define \(M_P=u_n^Pq-G_P\). The exact given residual is
\[
D_{u^P}=-2X_mM_P-M_P^2-B_{\rm cert}q^2.
\]

With
\[
r=\sqrt2-1,\qquad v=\frac{r^2}{1+2\sqrt2}<1/20,
\]
the literal enclosing-depth limits are
\[
X_m/S\to r,\quad q\to r,\quad
U_A/S\to(3+v)/4,\quad
G_A/S\to r/2+(1+v)/(4\sqrt2).
\]
Consequently
\[
\frac{M_A}{S}\to
\eta_A=r\left(\frac{43}{68}-\frac v4\right)
-\frac{1+v}{4\sqrt2}
>\frac{411}{6800}>\frac3{50}.
\]
The B comparison is exact on the **physical indices only**:
\[
0\le\rho_i^A-\rho_i^B
=\frac{T_i(S-1-T_i)}{2S(S+1+T_i)}
\le r^2/2,\qquad i<n.
\]
Here \(T_i\le S-h_{n-1}<S-1\) because \(h_{n-1}=1+a>1\). The ordering fails at the terminal \(\rho_n\), which is not used in this comparison; \(u_n\) is separately prescribed by the total-mass rule. If \(N_-=\sum_{i<n}h_i\max(-g_i,0)\), then
\[
M_B\ge M_A-(r^2/2)N_-,\qquad
\limsup N_-/S\le(1-r)/2.
\]
It follows that
\[
\liminf_{m\to\infty,\ m\ {\rm odd}}M_B/S
>\frac{207}{6800}>\frac3{100}.
\]
Both \(M_P\) are therefore positive for every sufficiently large odd enclosing \(m\), making both actual original-radius residuals strictly negative. The proof uses exact recurrences and uniform geometric tails, not a finite numerical sign or a free Gram direction.

## Original-goal boundary

The actual objective payoff satisfies
\[
S_m^2\frac{q^2}{X_m^2}\to1.
\]
It has order \(S_m^{-2}\). Thus the result rejects these particular dominance profiles because of their excessive terminal residual square; it does not attain the order-\(S_m^{-1}\) benchmark and does not disprove \(\mathcal W(H_m)\le1/B_{\rm cert}\). The exact original all-integer \(B_n\), normalization \(W=2R\), full function class, and selected original radius remain intact.

For these fixed profiles, a representation of \(D_u\) by nonnegative actual interpolation rows plus a PSD remainder is impossible if the current draft is correct. Other profiles and other certificates are not excluded.

## Second approach and Profile C

After the A/B counterexample was reported, the parent fixed Profile C, \(\rho_i=1-T_i/(8S)\), in R12_PROFILE_C_ADDENDUM_20261009.md. Its checked SHA-256 is 9001df1d3b8ecdbc198d8be2e85657610263fc93fdf8fd50f3e295f058e58030. Only the unused second approach was authorized.

The attempt used one actual regular triangle
\[
K=\operatorname{conv}\{(1,0),(-1/2,\sqrt3/2),(-1/2,-\sqrt3/2)\},
\quad F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\|g\|^2/2\},
\]
with selected minimum 0, and a proposed three-vertex cyclic suffix policy. For rotation \(R\) by \(2\pi/3\), \(w_j=R^{m-j}v_0\), and \(L_j=S_j-S_{j-1}\), its preassigned start is \(Z_1=\beta w_1\), \(Z_j=L_jw_j+Z_{j-1}\), \(x_0=Z_m\).

The necessary root bridge normal is \(n_m=Z_{m-1}+k_{m-1}v_0\). The draft derives
\[
\frac{\langle n_m,v_1-v_0\rangle}{S_{m-1}}
\longrightarrow \frac{3r^2}{2(1+r+r^2)}>0.
\]
The cyclic vertex table therefore violates the projection normal cone at macroscopic scale. The first genuine departure from vertex \(v_0\) occurs when the physical cumulative time exceeds an explicit threshold \(\tau_m\), with
\[
\tau_m/S_{m-1}\to1-r^2/(1+r+r^2)\in(0,1).
\]
This is a real binding change within the first child or at its endpoint. The subsequent actual triangular trajectory was not solved. No sign of \(D_{u^C}\), and no terminal value computed from the infeasible table, is claimed. Profile C is unresolved.

## Checks, files, counts, and stop

- Original fixed A/B profile SHA-256 matched 277b5d3e3473a358acf98ae9f10c64b063b462195da307c73c1ca0dd0d7439f4.
- Read the named complete sources, the operator profile, and complete original problem. The mathematical-writing skill was used for notation, source, and evidence checks.
- One native invocation; two substantive analytical approaches; mathematical programs 0; generated mathematical arrays 0; solvers 0; formal submissions 0; extra agents/models 0.
- Only PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md in this packet were created/edited. Administrative reading, text checks, and file hashing were used. No private worker memory, other R12 packet, shared administration, truth-store mutation, service change, Goal change, publication, or old grant was used.
- The parent received the actual A/B counterexample promptly and supplied the useful exact physical-index observation for Profile B. New mathematics remains UNVERIFIED despite independent agreement reported by the parent.
- Both approach slots are used. This call stops after final file readback and hashes. Parent owns any selective verification, ledger closeout, or later dispatch.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000555 -->
# R12 full-word primal obstruction to both fixed dominance profiles

**UNVERIFIED direct mathematical draft.** Grant `8298ccaea9198109`. This packet gives a complete proposed counterexample to the two fixed dominance functionals, for every sufficiently large odd enclosing depth. It does not resolve the original minimax problem. No verifier has reviewed the new mathematics.

Resource scope: one authorized native invocation; two substantive analytical approaches. The first is an exact nested, repeatedly reflecting trajectory of one static capped quadratic. The second, authorized by the later Profile C addendum, checks cyclic binding vertices in one regular triangular projection body and identifies the first actual loss of that vertex policy. No mathematical programs, arrays, solvers, extra agents, external model calls, or formal submissions were used. Read/write/hash commands are administrative only.

## 1. Locked sources and statement

The governing inputs were read: `OPERATOR.md`, the complete project `PROBLEM.md`, `R11_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md`, the complete `R12_FIXED_DOMINANCE_PROFILES_20261009.md`, and `R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md`. The task SHA-256 was checked as `04af5ae694d258315035fff2b470f3ec1a4d59f123e304572d4ab2c21b1774fe`; the fixed-profile SHA-256 was checked as `277b5d3e3473a358acf98ae9f10c64b063b462195da307c73c1ca0dd0d7439f4`. No other R12 packet or private worker memory was read.

For one enclosing integer \(m\ge1536\), throughout this proof set

\[
c_0=1+\sqrt2,\quad \epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad
\beta=1-\epsilon,\quad c=c_0(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\frac\beta c,
\]
\[
H_0=(),\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j),\qquad
N_j=2^j-1,\quad S_j=\sum H_j.
\]

All children use these same enclosing-\(m\) parameters. Put \(n=N_m\), \(S=S_m\), \(h=H_m\), and \(T_i=\sum_{t<i}h_t\). The certificate total, distinguished from the original all-integer benchmark \(B_n\), is

\[
B_{\rm cert}=1+\frac{32}{17}S.
\]

For \(P=A,B\), the fixed weights are

\[
\rho_i^A=1-\frac{T_i}{2S},\qquad
\rho_i^B=\frac{S+1}{S+1+T_i},\qquad
u_i^P=\rho_i^P h_i\quad(i<n),
\]
\[
U_P=\sum_{i<n}u_i^P,\qquad u_n^P=B_{\rm cert}-U_P>0.
\]

**Proposed conclusion.** There is an integer \(M\ge1536\) such that, for every odd \(m\ge M\), the static function and actual full-\(H_m\) trajectory constructed below have

\[
D_{u^A}<0,\qquad D_{u^B}<0.
\]

These are counterexamples to the *specified profile inequalities*, including their original selected minimizer and original radius. The actual objective payoff of the witnesses is of order \(S^{-2}\), well below the order \(S^{-1}\) benchmark. The conclusion consequently does not disprove an upper bound on \(\mathcal W(H_m)\), or settle the original limit \(\mathcal W_n^*/U_n^{\rm ZJ}\).

## 2. One static smooth convex function

Use the same one-dimensional function for every \(m\):

\[
F(x)=
\begin{cases}
x^2/2,& |x|\le1,\\
|x|-1/2,& |x|\ge1.
\end{cases}
\qquad g(x)=F'(x)=\operatorname{clip}(x,-1,1).
\tag{2.1}
\]

The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz, so \(F\) is convex and 1-smooth. Its unique minimizer is \(x_\star=0\), with \(F_\star=0\). At every real \(x\),

\[
2F(x)=2xg(x)-g(x)^2.
\tag{2.2}
\]

Thus all actual minimizer interpolation slacks satisfy

\[
Q_{\star,i}=-2F(x_i)+2g_i x_i-g_i^2=0.
\tag{2.3}
\]

The one scalar path changes its saturated sign at each nested suffix, on the entire physical word, and enters the quadratic interior at the last physical query and at the actual terminal query. Its terminal gradient is retained in \(D_u\). This repeated-reflection trajectory is not a globally affine Huber run or a bank of independent one-reflection components.

## 3. The starting point and all physical iterates

Define \(k_j=ac^j\), and define positive starts by

\[
X_1=\beta,
\qquad
X_j=S_{j-1}+1+k_{j-1}-X_{j-1}\quad(2\le j\le m).
\tag{3.1}
\]

The original start is \(x_0=X_m\). We first establish that this really generates the claimed full-word trajectory at the literal enclosing parameters.

Let

\[
A=\frac{a}{c-2}=\frac{\beta}{c(c-2)}.
\]

Summing the word recursion gives, for all \(j\ge0\),

\[
S_j+1=Ac^j+(1-A)2^j.
\tag{3.2}
\]

Since \(c>c_0\), \(c(c-2)>1\); hence \(0<A<\beta<1\). For \(j\ge2\), put

\[
\delta_j=k_j-(S_{j-1}+1)
=(\beta-A)c^{j-1}-(1-A)2^{j-1}.
\tag{3.3}
\]

Writing \(r=\sqrt2-1=1/c_0\), the first such difference is exactly

\[
\delta_2=\beta c-2-a
=\frac{\epsilon[2r-c_0\epsilon(1+\epsilon)]}{1+\epsilon}>0.
\tag{3.4}
\]

For the displayed positivity it is enough that \(\epsilon\le1/16\), which holds here: \(r>2/5\), \(c_0<5/2\), and \(c_0\epsilon(1+\epsilon)\le85/512<4/5<2r\). Also,

\[
\delta_{j+1}=c\delta_j+(c-2)(1-A)2^{j-1}>\delta_j>0.
\tag{3.5}
\]

Let \(E_j=k_j-X_j\). Then \(E_1=0\), because \(k_1=\beta\), and (3.1) gives

\[
E_j=\delta_j-E_{j-1}.
\tag{3.6}
\]

Induction using (3.5) gives \(0\le E_j\le\delta_j\) for every \(j\ge2\), with \(E_j>0\) there. In particular,

\[
X_j=S_{j-1}+1+E_{j-1}\ge S_{j-1}+1
\quad(j\ge2),\qquad X_j\le k_j.
\tag{3.7}
\]

Start \(H_j\), \(j\ge2\), at \(X_j\). Every query in its first \(H_{j-1}\) has gradient \(+1\): the endpoint of this first child is \(X_j-S_{j-1}=1+E_{j-1}\ge1\). The bridge query also has gradient \(+1\); its physical step \(1+k_{j-1}\) sends that endpoint to

\[
1+E_{j-1}-(1+k_{j-1})=-X_{j-1}.
\tag{3.8}
\]

Oddness of \(g\) implies that the remaining \(H_{j-1}\) is the sign-reversed trajectory starting at \(X_{j-1}\). At the bottom, \(H_1=(1+a)\) starts at \(X_1=\beta\in(0,1)\) and ends at \(-a\beta\). Define

\[
q=a\beta\in(0,1).
\]

The terminal point and terminal gradient of the complete run are therefore exactly

\[
x_n=g_n=(-1)^m q,\qquad F(x_n)=q^2/2.
\tag{3.9}
\]

For completeness, the preceding recursive description specifies *every physical sample* without dropping repeated queries. Let \(I_j=n-N_j\), so that the final nested \(H_j\) starts at physical index \(I_j\). For \(j=m,m-1,\ldots,2\), set

\[
\sigma_j=(-1)^{m-j},\qquad
L_j=S_j-S_{j-1}=S_{j-1}+1+k_{j-1}.
\]

For each physical index \(I_j\le i<I_{j-1}\), put \(p_{j,i}=\sum_{t=I_j}^{i-1}h_t\). Then

\[
x_i=\sigma_j(X_j-p_{j,i}),\quad g_i=\sigma_j,
\quad F(x_i)=|x_i|-1/2.
\tag{3.10}
\]

The last physical sample, at \(i=n-1=I_1\), is

\[
x_{n-1}=g_{n-1}=(-1)^{m-1}\beta,
\qquad F(x_{n-1})=\beta^2/2,
\tag{3.11}
\]

followed by (3.9). Each block in (3.10) consists of the first child and the bridge of its actual suffix; it contains \(2^{j-1}\) physical steps, and its mass is \(L_j\). Together with the final physical step these blocks have exactly \(2^m-1\) steps and mass \(S_m\).

The original radius is \(R_0^2=X_m^2\). There is no later choice of minimizer, body, scale, or child radius.

## 4. Literal enclosing-depth asymptotics

All limits in this section are along \(m\to\infty\) with the literal parameters \(\epsilon_m=1/(4m)\). No fixed-child-depth limit is substituted for this enclosing-depth limit.

Solving (3.1) with (3.2) gives the exact expression

\[
X_m=\frac{A+a}{c+1}c^m+\frac{1-A}{3}2^m
+(-1)^{m-1}\left[\beta-\frac{(A+a)c}{c+1}-\frac{2(1-A)}3\right].
\tag{4.1}
\]

Since \(c\to c_0>2\), \(A\to1\), and \(a\to r\), equations (3.2) and (4.1) imply

\[
S_m\to\infty,\qquad \frac{X_m}{S_m}\longrightarrow
\frac{c_0-1}{c_0+1}=r,\qquad q\longrightarrow r.
\tag{4.2}
\]

Let \(V_j=\sum_{h\in H_j}h^2\). Direct summation of \(V_j=2V_{j-1}+(1+ac^{j-1})^2\) gives

\[
V_j=(2^j-1)
+2a\frac{c^j-2^j}{c-2}
+a^2\frac{c^{2j}-2^j}{c^2-2}.
\tag{4.3}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\frac{V_m}{S_m^2}\longrightarrow
v:=\frac{r^2}{1+2\sqrt2},\qquad 0<v<\frac1{20}.
\tag{4.4}
\]

The last inequality is equivalent to \(59<42\sqrt2\), whose square is \(3481<3528\).

## 5. Exact evaluation of Profile A on the full path

The unsigned physical mass identity fixed by the source gives

\[
U_A=\frac{3S}{4}+\frac{V_m}{4S},
\qquad
\frac{U_A}{S}\longrightarrow\frac{3+v}{4}.
\tag{5.1}
\]

Define the actual signed physical gradient sum \(G_A=\sum_{i<n}u_i^A g_i\). For any block of physical steps of mass \(L\), squared-step sum \(V_{\rm block}\), and starting cumulative time \(t\),

\[
\sum_{\rm block}h_iT_i=tL+\frac{L^2-V_{\rm block}}2.
\tag{5.2}
\]

For the actual block \(j\ge2\), \(t=S-S_j\), \(L=L_j=S_j-S_{j-1}\), and \(V_{\rm block}=V_j-V_{j-1}\). Hence its unsigned Profile A weight is exactly

\[
W_j=\frac{L_j}{2}
+\frac{S_j^2-S_{j-1}^2+V_j-V_{j-1}}{4S}.
\tag{5.3}
\]

The same expression with \(S_0=V_0=0\) defines the last single-step weight \(W_1\). All block gradients are their signs except for the final physical gradient, whose magnitude is \(\beta\). Thus

\[
G_A=\sum_{j=1}^m(-1)^{m-j}W_j
+(-1)^{m-1}(\beta-1)W_1.
\tag{5.4}
\]

The correction in (5.4), divided by \(S\), tends to zero. For each fixed \(k\ge0\), (3.2) and (4.3) give

\[
\frac{S_{m-k}}S\to r^k,\qquad
\frac{V_{m-k}}{S^2}\to vr^{2k}.
\]

Accordingly,

\[
\frac{W_{m-k}}S\to
\frac{1-r}{2}r^k+\frac{(1+v)(1-r^2)}4r^{2k}.
\tag{5.5}
\]

Here the passage from fixed \(k\) to the entire alternating sum is uniform. Indeed, for all sufficiently large enclosing \(m\) and \(0\le j\le m\), (3.2) and (4.3) give \(S_j\le Cc^j\), \(V_j\le Cc^{2j}\), and \(S_m\ge c^m/C\), for one absolute constant \(C\). Formula (5.3) then bounds \(|W_{m-k}|/S\) by \(C'(r^k+r^{2k})\), because \(c\ge c_0\). The tails beyond \(k=K\) tend to zero uniformly as \(K\to\infty\). Taking a finite-prefix limit and then this geometric tail bound justifies summing (5.5).

The resulting limit is

\[
\frac{G_A}{S}\longrightarrow
\gamma_A:=\frac{1-r}{2(1+r)}
+\frac{(1+v)(1-r^2)}{4(1+r^2)}
=\frac r2+\frac{1+v}{4\sqrt2}.
\tag{5.6}
\]

Use odd \(m\), so that the actual terminal gradient is \(-q\). Define the positive-direction residual increment

\[
M_A=u_n^Aq-G_A.
\]

By (4.2), (5.1), and (5.6),

\[
\frac{M_A}{S}\longrightarrow
\eta_A:=r\left(\frac{32}{17}-\frac{3+v}{4}\right)-\gamma_A
=r\left(\frac{43}{68}-\frac v4\right)
-\frac{1+v}{4\sqrt2}.
\tag{5.7}
\]

This constant is strictly positive with a rational margin. Since \(r>2/5\), \(v<1/20\), and \(1/\sqrt2<5/7\),

\[
\eta_A>
\frac25\left(\frac{43}{68}-\frac1{80}\right)
-\frac{21}{20}\frac5{28}
=\frac{411}{6800}>\frac3{50}.
\tag{5.8}
\]

Therefore \(M_A>0\) for every sufficiently large odd enclosing \(m\).

## 6. Profile B: an exact comparison on physical indices

The \(S+1\) in Profile B is retained. Its terminal \(\rho_n\) is not used in the comparison of physical sums below.

For \(0\le t\le1\), define

\[
\rho_\infty^B(t)=\frac1{1+t},\qquad
d(t)=1-\frac t2-\frac1{1+t}
=\frac{t(1-t)}{2(1+t)}.
\]

An elementary derivative check gives

\[
0\le d(t)\le d_*:=\frac{r^2}{2},
\tag{6.1}
\]

with maximum at \(t=r\). For the literal profiles, direct subtraction gives

\[
\rho_i^A-\rho_i^B
=\frac{T_i(S-1-T_i)}{2S(S+1+T_i)}.
\tag{6.2}
\]

For every physical index \(i<n\), the last physical step is \(h_{n-1}=1+a>1\), so \(T_i\le S-(1+a)<S-1\). Thus (6.2) is nonnegative at all the physical indices. Also \(\rho_i^B\ge1/(1+T_i/S)\), and hence

\[
0\le\rho_i^A-\rho_i^B\le d(T_i/S)\le d_*
\qquad(i<n).
\]

This ordering is false at \(i=n\), where \(\rho_n^B>1/2=\rho_n^A\). The terminal weight is separately fixed by \(u_n=B_{\rm cert}-U_P\); terminal \(\rho_n\) only parameterizes the auxiliary \(\ell_n\) in the parent matrix representation and is not used here.

Applying the physical-index inequalities to the actual gradients gives

\[
U_B\le U_A,
\qquad
G_B:=\sum_{i<n}u_i^Bg_i\le G_A+d_*N_-,
\tag{6.3}
\]

where \(N_-:=\sum_{i<n}h_i\max(-g_i,0)\) is the physical negative-gradient mass. The exact displacement identity is \(\sum_{i<n}h_i g_i=X_m-x_n\), so

\[
N_-=
\frac{\sum_{i<n}h_i|g_i|-\sum_{i<n}h_i g_i}{2}
\le\frac{S-X_m+x_n}{2}.
\tag{6.4}
\]

For odd \(m\), \(x_n=-q\), and in particular

\[
\limsup_{m\to\infty,\ m\ {\rm odd}}\frac{N_-}{S}
\le\frac{1-r}{2}.
\tag{6.5}
\]

Define \(M_B=u_n^Bq-G_B\). Equations (6.3) give the finite-depth inequality

\[
M_B\ge M_A-d_*N_-.
\tag{6.6}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\liminf_{m\to\infty,\ m\ {\rm odd}}\frac{M_B}{S}
\ge \eta_A-\frac{r^2(1-r)}4
>\frac{207}{6800}>\frac3{100}.
\tag{6.7}
\]

For the last strict inequality, \(r>2/5\) and \(r^2=3-2\sqrt2<1/5\) give \(r^2(1-r)/4<3/100\), while (5.8) gives \(\eta_A>411/6800\). Thus \(M_B>0\) for every sufficiently large odd enclosing \(m\), with the \(S+1\) in the fixed profile fully retained. The parent independently identified the physical-index restriction used in (6.2).

## 7. The actual original-radius residual is negative

For either profile, (2.3) and (3.9) reduce its *given* doubled residual exactly to

\[
D_{u^P}=X_m^2-B_{\rm cert}q^2
-\left(X_m-G_P-u_n^Pg_n\right)^2.
\tag{7.1}
\]

When \(m\) is odd, \(g_n=-q\), and hence

\[
X_m-G_P-u_n^Pg_n=X_m+M_P.
\]

For every sufficiently large odd \(m\), Sections 5 and 6 give \(X_m>0\) and \(M_P>0\). Therefore

\[
\boxed{\quad D_{u^P}=-2X_mM_P-M_P^2-B_{\rm cert}q^2<0,
\qquad P=A,B.\quad}
\tag{7.2}
\]

For A the normalized limiting residual is explicitly

\[
\frac{D_{u^A}}{S^2}\longrightarrow
r^2-(r+\eta_A)^2<0
\qquad(m\to\infty\text{ through odd integers}).
\tag{7.3}
\]

For B, (6.7) already implies the strict bound

\[
\limsup_{m\to\infty,\ m\ {\rm odd}}
\frac{D_{u^B}}{S^2}
\le r^2-\left(r+\frac3{100}\right)^2<0.
\tag{7.4}
\]

An explicit numerical depth threshold is unnecessary for this all-sufficiently-large-odd-depth counterexample: the displayed exact formulas and uniform geometric tail estimates prove existence of a finite threshold. No finite computation or freely assigned Gram direction supplies the sign.

## 8. Original payoff and exact scope

The actual witness value, with the original selected minimum and radius, is

\[
\frac{2(F(x_n)-F_\star)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2}
=\frac{q^2}{X_m^2},
\qquad
S_m^2\frac{q^2}{X_m^2}\longrightarrow1.
\tag{8.1}
\]

Thus this family rejects the two dominance decompositions because their large terminal weight produces an excessive residual square. Its original objective payoff is only order \(S_m^{-2}\). It does not supply a full-class worst-case upper certificate, a benchmark-scale lower witness, or a resolution of the original minimax comparison. In particular, failure of these \(D_u\) profiles does not imply failure of \(\mathcal W(H_m)\le1/B_{\rm cert}\).

The conclusion does rule out constructing a nonnegative-interpolation-plus-PSD proof of either fixed \(D_u\) on all original trajectories, provided the identities in the parent profile note are used without changing the profiles. On the actual vectors above, such a representation would have a nonnegative right side, contradicting (7.2). It says nothing about a different profile or a different upper-bound certificate.

## 9. Audit register and disposition

| --- | --- | --- |

## 10. Second approach: a binding triangular body, with Profile C unresolved

The parent subsequently fixed Profile C in the complete addendum
R12_PROFILE_C_ADDENDUM_20261009.md, whose checked SHA-256 is
9001df1d3b8ecdbc198d8be2e85657610263fc93fdf8fd50f3e295f058e58030.
Its new physical profile is \(\rho_i^C=1-T_i/(8S)\), with the same \(B_{\rm cert}\) and terminal mass rule. The parent authorized only the previously unused second approach. The addendum already establishes that the critical orbit above does not reject C; its re-evaluation is not counted as a different approach.

The distinct geometry attempted here is the single compact triangle

\[
v_0=(1,0),\quad v_1=(-1/2,\sqrt3/2),\quad
v_2=(-1/2,-\sqrt3/2),\qquad K=\operatorname{conv}\{v_0,v_1,v_2\}.
\]

Use the static function

\[
F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\|g\|^2/2\},
\qquad \nabla F_K(x)=P_K(x).
\tag{10.1}
\]

The maximizer is the unique Euclidean projection. Convexity follows from the supremum of affine functions of \(x\). Projection's variational inequalities give
\(\|P_K(x)-P_K(y)\|^2\le\langle P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y\rangle\), hence a 1-Lipschitz gradient. The usual upper and lower comparisons at the two maximizers give differentiability with the displayed gradient. Since 0 is interior to \(K\), every \(x\ne0\) has \(F_K(x)>0\) by testing a sufficiently small positive multiple of \(x\); thus the original minimizer is uniquely 0. Equation (2.3) again holds at every actual sample of this function.

Let \(R\) denote rotation by \(2\pi/3\). For one enclosing \(m\), let

\[
w_j=R^{m-j}v_0,\qquad
Z_1=\beta w_1,\qquad
Z_j=L_jw_j+Z_{j-1}\quad(2\le j\le m),
\tag{10.2}
\]

where \(L_j=S_j-S_{j-1}\) is the exact physical block mass used earlier. The actual candidate start is \(x_0=Z_m\), selected before the forward run on the fixed body \(K\). Its original radius is \(\|Z_m\|^2\).

The proposed cyclic policy would keep projection \(w_j\) throughout the first child and bridge of suffix \(H_j\), and then enter the next suffix at \(Z_{j-1}\). It is a policy about actual vertices of one triangle, not independent scalar components. Crucially, it requires the bridge-query normal

\[
n_j=Z_{j-1}+k_{j-1}w_j
\]

to belong to \(N_K(w_j)\). This condition is necessary because the proposed bridge query is \(Z_{j-1}+(1+k_{j-1})w_j\). It is also sufficient for all earlier queries in that block, since their normals add nonnegative multiples of \(w_j\), and
\(\langle w_j,v-w_j\rangle\le0\) for all \(v\in K\).

This normal-cone condition fails at the root at an order-one scale. Put \(r=\sqrt2-1\), and represent the plane as the complex plane with \(v_0=1\), \(Rv_0=z=-1/2+i\sqrt3/2\). The same geometric-tail argument as in Section 5, now for the vector sum (10.2), gives

\[
\frac{Z_{m-1}}{S_{m-1}}\longrightarrow
(1-r)\frac{z}{1-rz},\qquad
\frac{k_{m-1}}{S_{m-1}}\longrightarrow r.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\frac{n_m}{S_{m-1}}\longrightarrow
\nu:=r+(1-r)\frac{z}{1-rz}
=\frac{r(1+z)}{1-rz}
=\frac{r}{1+r+r^2}
\left(\frac{1-r}{2},\frac{\sqrt3(1+r)}{2}\right).
\tag{10.3}
\]

Here \(1-r-r^2=r\) was used. The normal row toward the next vertex has the strictly wrong sign:

\[
\left\langle\nu,v_1-v_0\right\rangle
=\frac{3r^2}{2(1+r+r^2)}>0.
\tag{10.4}
\]

Thus the proposed cyclic vertex table cannot be the actual complete GD trajectory for all sufficiently large enclosing \(m\). This is a geometric feasibility failure, not a negative value of \(D_{u^C}\).

The first binding event in the genuine forward run can also be located, without replacing any gradients. While the projection stays \(v_0\), the exact queries are \(Z_m-T_i v_0\). The supporting row for \(v_1\) first leaves the vertex normal cone when

\[
T_i>\tau_m:=-\frac23\langle Z_m,v_1-v_0\rangle-1
=S_{m-1}+k_{m-1}-\frac23\langle Z_{m-1},v_1-v_0\rangle.
\tag{10.5}
\]

Its normalized position satisfies

\[
\frac{\tau_m}{S_{m-1}}\longrightarrow
1-\frac{r^2}{1+r+r^2}\in(0,1).
\tag{10.6}
\]

For sufficiently large \(m\), the start has a positive vertical component; the competing \(v_2\) row crosses later than the \(v_1\) row. Also \(\tau_m>0\). It follows that the actual path agrees with the \(v_0\) policy up to the first physical index with \(T_i>\tau_m\), and at that actual query its projection differs from \(v_0\). Such a physical index exists no later than the root bridge query, whose cumulative mass is \(S_{m-1}>\tau_m\). Equality \(T_i=\tau_m\) still has projection \(v_0\). The path therefore encounters a genuine binding change involving another edge or vertex inside the first child or at its endpoint.

The subsequent actual projection path was not solved in this bounded approach. No claimed terminal vector, original-radius value, or sign of \(D_{u^C}\) is inferred from the rejected cyclic table. This second approach supplies a precise obstruction to that simple triangular inverse construction, while Profile C remains unresolved. Its outcome does not alter the complete A/B counterexample in Sections 1--8. Both substantive approach slots are now used, and no continuation or additional model is authorized by this packet.
